# Sprint 2 Review Feedback — round 1 (cycle-126, global sprint 248)

**Reviewer:** Senior Tech Lead Reviewer Agent (Fable 5.1 lead, unattended `/run sprint-plan`) with two-voice cross-model dissent (`adversarial-review.sh --type review`: primary `gpt-5.5-pro` → `codex-headless` (keyless inner walk), companion `claude-headless` — the companion voice this very sprint shipped, run per focused chunk: `a-dissent-script`, `b-contracts`, `c-tests-fixtures`)
**Date:** 2026-09-25
**Sprint Reference:** grimoires/loa/sprint.md — Sprint 2
**Implementation Report:** grimoires/loa/a2a/sprint-248/reviewer.md

---

## Overall Assessment

The sprint delivers the four FR-2 pieces test-first (companion voice, tolerant schema, rejected-payload contract, repair model) and its AC Verification walks every Sprint 2 criterion verbatim (`validate-ac-verification.sh --sprint-id sprint-2` exit 0). The first dissent run with two live voices worked as designed and found what a single voice had not: the primary voice raised one BLOCKING on the rejected-payload contract, and the companion voice (`claude-headless`, 8–9 min per chunk on this host) raised twenty advisories across the three chunks, sixteen of them real. Three are HIGH — the contract check that only looks for a heading, an unguarded fold that can blank the paid-for primary envelope, and a primary-chain exhaustion that buries a completed companion — and must be fixed before approval. Every finding below was reproduced by a failing test before its fix.

**Verdict:** CHANGES REQUIRED

---

## Changes Required

### 1. The rejected-payload contract (chunk b DISS-001 BLOCKING; chunk b C-001, chunk c C-004 say the same)

- **HIGH** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/verdict-derive.sh:rejected_summary_check` — the check only proves the `## Rejected dissent payloads` heading exists; a bare heading, or one bullet for three rejected rows, passes.
**File:** `.claude/scripts/verdict-derive.sh`
**Issue:** the documented contract (D-2.3, `resources/ADVERSARIAL-REVIEW.md`) requires one triage line per `metadata.rejected_summary[]` entry. Heading-only enforcement leaves the loss the feature exists to prevent one line further down — the same one-way gap the LOA-VERDICT trailer rule was introduced to close. Dissent primary DISS-001 (BLOCKING, anchor valid); companion b C-001 and c C-004 independently.
**Why This Matters:** "a dropped finding is never silently lost" is prose-only until the gate counts.
**Required Fix:** count the list items between the heading and the next `## ` heading and require `count ≥ n` (`n = rejected_summary | length`); name both numbers in the violation. Tests: heading-only → exit 1; two rows, one bullet → exit 1; two rows, two bullets → exit 0.

### 2. The fold can blank the primary envelope (chunk a C-002)

- **HIGH** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh:main` — `result=$(_fold_companion …)` is unguarded; any jq failure inside the fold makes `result` the empty string and every later step pipes nothing through jq.
**File:** `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh`
**Issue:** the completed, paid-for primary envelope is silently replaced by empty output. The diff already uses the safe capture-and-validate pattern for `rejected_summary` but not here.
**Why This Matters:** a metadata shape change or a non-object finding in the companion's payload would turn a successful dissent into an empty file that the COMPLETED gate reads as missing.
**Required Fix:** capture into a temporary, require non-empty JSON with a `.metadata` object, otherwise keep the unfolded result with `companion_voice: {planned: true, status: "fold_failed"}` and a log line. Test: a fold stubbed to print garbage → `status reviewed`, one finding, `companion_voice.status fold_failed`.

### 3. A primary chain that exhausts buries the companion (chunk a C-006)

- **HIGH** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh:_fold_companion` — when the primary ends `api_failure` / `malformed_response` and the companion completes, its findings are appended to an envelope downstream treats as a failed dissent.
**File:** `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh`
**Issue:** the asymmetry contradicts the sprint's own contract ("nothing dropped"): a companion failure is modelled as a dropped voice, a primary failure drops the companion's success.
**Why This Matters:** on a keyless host with a broken `codex` install, every review would report a failed dissent although a full second voice answered.
**Required Fix:** in the success branch, if the primary's status is a failure status, promote the envelope to `status: reviewed`, `degraded: true`, record `primary_voice: {status: failed, model, error}` and a `status_note`; verdict quality shows one voice of two succeeded. Test: all three primary hops `unavailable`, companion ok → `reviewed`, `degraded true`, `primary_voice.status failed`, one finding with `voice claude-headless`, `voices_succeeded_ids == [claude-headless]`.

### 4. The second voice's lifecycle and accounting (chunk a C-001, C-004, C-005, C-007, C-003, C-010; chunk b C-002, C-003; chunk c C-001, C-003)

- **MEDIUM** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh:main` — the companion is forked before the primary walk and only reaped by an unbounded `wait`; no EXIT/INT/TERM trap kills it, so a Ctrl-C, an early exit, or a hung hop leaves it running and paying (C-001; chunk c C-001 notes the harness cannot see an orphan because `$( )` blocks on the pipe's writers).
**Required Fix:** register INT/TERM/EXIT traps right after the fork, reap the whole process tree (the hop's own children escape a parent-only kill), and cap the wait at chain length × per-call timeout + 30 s (`LOA_ADVERSARIAL_COMPANION_WAIT_SECONDS` pins it) with `failure_class: timeout`. Test: a 4 s hop under a 1 s cap → `timeout`, the review completes, no `sleep` survives.
- **MEDIUM** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh:_fold_companion` — `metadata.cost_usd` / tokens stay the primary's; the companion's spend is visible only under `companion_voice.cost_cents`, and `budget_cents` is applied to the primary alone (C-004).
**Required Fix:** sum both voices into the envelope totals, keep the per-voice figure under `companion_voice`, and document `budget_cents` as a per-voice cap. Test: two `0.0123` voices → `cost_usd 0.0246`, `tokens_input 200`, `cost_cents 1.23`.
- **MEDIUM** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh:main` — the companion chain is never de-duplicated against `fallback_chain`, so a mixed-family primary chain can run the same CLI twice and report it as cross-family consensus; independence is never recorded (C-005; chunk c C-003 adds that cheval's *inner* chain can land on the companion's family after the fact).
**Required Fix:** after the walk set `companion_voice.independent` from the families of the voices that actually answered — the primary's succeeded id from its last verdict-quality sidecar, not its configured hop — record `primary_succeeded_model`, and when the two envelopes are one family keep the companion's findings (tagged) but let it contribute no voice to verdict quality (`counted_as: duplicate_voice`; the aggregator counts distinct voices and its INV-5 forbids one id both succeeded and dropped) with a stderr warning. Hops the primary chain also holds stay in the companion chain and are listed under `shared_hops`. *Live re-run note:* the first cut removed shared hops before the fork (`no_disjoint_route` when nothing remained) — on this very host that removed the companion from every dissent, because the configured primary chain ends on `claude-headless` as its last resort; the pre-filter was withdrawn the same round. Tests: a chain holding `claude-headless` → still planned, `shared_hops`, independent when the primary answers from its own family; a primary whose inner walk lands on `claude-headless` while the companion is `claude-headless` → `independent false`, one distinct voice in verdict quality, both findings kept; the honest case → `true`.
- **MEDIUM** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh:_companion_chain` — the CLI hop is always planned, so a single-family host plans a companion that cannot succeed on every run and, for the audit gate, turns a clean single-voice audit into a permanently degraded one after upgrade (C-007; chunk b C-003 raises the same upgrade consequence and the doubled spend).
**Required Fix:** plan the HTTP voice only with a credential and the CLI hop only with its binary on PATH; with neither, `planned: false, reason: no_route`. Keep the default `true` (two voices is the sprint's point) but say so in the upgrade note with the spend and the opt-out. Tests: no key, no CLI → `no_route`; the CLI alone is a route.
- **MEDIUM** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh:_walk_companion_chain` — the companion runs `>/dev/null 2>&1`; `failure_class` is reconstructed from the exit code alone and an operator has no diagnostic (C-003).
**Required Fix:** log to `<workdir>/companion/companion.log` and carry the last non-empty line, redacted, as `companion_voice.last_error`; mask the bare provider-key shapes (`sk-…`, `xai-…`, `gsk_…`, `AIza…`) the shared redactor does not cover. Test: a hop that prints a fake `sk-ant-…` token → `last_error` keeps the message and not the token; the token is nowhere in the envelope.
- **MEDIUM** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh:_fold_companion` — the companion's rejected rows land in its `-companion.jsonl` sidecar but the fold drops that path; the reviewer contract sends the triager to a file that does not hold the rows they must triage (C-010).
**Required Fix:** `companion_voice.rejected_sidecar` names the companion file when a row landed in it and is `null` otherwise (the empty file removed); rows carry their `voice`; the resource doc says so. Tests: a reject in the companion → the path, one row whose `reject_reason` matches the summary and `model claude-headless`; no reject → `null`.
- **MEDIUM** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/verdict-derive.sh:rejected_summary_check` — every failure path is fail-open: an explicit `--envelope` that does not exist WARNs and passes (unlike `--review-file`), an unparseable envelope or a non-array `rejected_summary` WARNs and passes; `--envelope` is in Usage but not in Options (chunk b C-002).
**Required Fix:** an explicit missing `--envelope` is a usage error (exit 2, at parse time); an existing-but-unparseable sibling envelope, or a non-array summary, is a violation; add the Options entry. Tests for both.

### 5. Smaller items (chunk a C-008, C-009, C-011; chunk c C-001, C-002, C-005, C-006)

- **LOW** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh:_adv_cred_present` — the `grep | tail | sed` pipeline materialises the secret in `val` before testing `-n`; under xtrace the value would echo (C-008). **Fix:** a presence-only `grep -Eq`; test NRM-8 runs the probe under `set -x` and asserts the value never appears.
- **LOW** (confidence: medium) `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh:_walk_companion_chain` — both walks shared `$_ADVERSARIAL_WORKDIR` (C-009); the prompt files are read-only and every intermediate is keyed by attempt/model, so no race was found, but the companion now works in its own `companion/` sub-workdir with its log and sidecars there.
- **LOW** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh:_companion_chain` — the companion chain was hard-coded while the primary's is configurable, and with a key present it leads with the KF-002/KF-011 class (C-011). **Fix:** optional `flatline_protocol.<block>.companion_chain: {anthropic: […], openai: […]}`, used as given; the example config documents it. Test CMP-13.
- **LOW** (confidence: high) `tests/unit/adversarial-review-companion.bats` — no mirror of CMP-2 (every primary hop failing while the companion succeeds) and no companion walking its own chain (chunk c C-001). **Fix:** CMP-10 (primary exhausted, companion ok) and CMP-6/CMP-13 (a key present → the chain starts at the HTTP voice; an operator chain is walked as given).
- **LOW** (confidence: high) `tests/unit/adversarial-review-companion.bats:setup` — the stub spoke the review vocabulary to the audit gate, so CMP-7's finding went through reject → repair (through the stubbed `invoke_dissenter`, not a live model — the "dials a real model" half is refuted: the repair round-trip calls `invoke_dissenter`, which the harness replaces) (chunk c C-002). **Fix:** the stub emits `MEDIUM` for `type == audit`; CMP-7 asserts `rejected_count 0`, one `MEDIUM` finding.
- **LOW** (confidence: high) `tests/unit/*.bats` — GNU-only `sed -i` (no suffix, `0,/re/` address, `\n` in the replacement) in CMP-4/CMP-5 (chunk c C-005), and the failure-class taxonomy pinned only to codes the test author chose, `model_unavailable` uncovered (chunk c C-006). **Fix:** a `_cfg_edit` python helper replaces every `sed -i`; CMP-17 pins the classifier to cheval's `EXIT_CODES` (4 `MISSING_API_KEY` → `auth`, 6 `BUDGET_EXCEEDED` → `quota`, 3/124 → `timeout`, 5 `INVALID_RESPONSE` → `malformed`, 1 and others → `model_unavailable`; `wait_timeout` → `timeout`) and each class to its verdict-quality drop reason; the resource doc states the mapping. NRM-4's `wc -l` comparison is already whitespace-safe under `[ = ]` on Linux; left as is.
- **LOW** (confidence: high) `tests/unit/adversarial-review-repair-loop.bats` — reviewer's own find while widening the regression set: three C14 cases (repair succeeds / repair mutates a non-violated field / repair unavailable) have failed since the Sprint 2 commit, because the tolerant normaliser now derives an empty `failure_mode` from the description before validation and the repair round-trip they test is never reached; the suite was missing from Sprint 2's regression list. **Fix:** the three cases bypass the derivation the way NRM-7 does and keep exercising the repair path; the suite joins the regression set.

### 6. Second two-voice run, on the round-1 commit (chunk b C-001 … C-005; chunk c C-001 … C-006; chunk a: one primary BLOCKING, refuted below)

The re-run itself taught two things the unit harness could not: the round-1 disjoint pre-filter removed the companion on this host (the configured primary chain ends on `claude-headless`), and the companion's `claude -p` hop timed out at cheval's 610 s while the Sprint 3 baseline eval shared the CLI (384 s alone). Both are fixed; the second run then had two voices on chunks b and c.

- **MEDIUM** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/verdict-derive.sh` — usage errors exited 2, the code the header documents as "no trailer, legacy pass", and printed nothing under `--json`; a mistyped `--envelope` read as a pass to any consumer honouring the contract (chunk b C-002). **Fix:** `usage_error` exits 1 and, with `--json`, emits a result object (`consistent: false`, `usage_error: true`); the five usage tests and VO-8 retarget to 1; `golden-path.sh` (any rc ≠ 0 denies) keeps working and its comment says so.
- **MEDIUM** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/verdict-derive.sh:rejected_summary_check` — the bullet count took indented sub-bullets, so one entry with two sub-bullets covered three payloads; a non-array `rejected_summary` was counted (a string by its length) (chunk b C-003). **Fix:** only column-0 bullets count; a present non-array is its own violation (`of type string`); tests for both.
- **MEDIUM** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/verdict-derive.sh:rejected_summary_check` — only the last run's envelope was enforced: a chunked dissent overwrites `adversarial-<gate>.json`, and a companion whose fold failed never reaches `rejected_summary`, while the sidecar rows survive unread (chunk b C-004). **Fix:** the rows of every `adversarial-rejected-<gate>*.jsonl` beside the envelope count too (`need = max(summary, rows)`, the violation names the source); test with a `-companion` file and a per-chunk file, and the audit gate ignoring the review's sidecars.
- **LOW** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/verdict-derive.sh` — `ENVELOPE_FILE` was the one option not initialised, so an exported variable of that name posed as `--envelope`; `ENVELOPE_EXPLICIT` was written and never read (chunk b C-005). **Fix:** initialised with the others; `--json` reports `envelope` and `envelope_explicit`.
- **LOW** (confidence: high) `.loa.config.yaml.example` — the `companion_chain` comment still described the withdrawn pre-filter ("is dropped") (chunk b C-001). **Fix:** it now states `shared_hops` and post-walk independence.
- **LOW** (confidence: high) `tests/unit/adversarial-review-companion.bats:CMP-14` — `! pgrep -f "sleep 4"` matches any process on the host, and passes vacuously without `pgrep` (chunk c C-001). **Fix:** the hung stub runs under a PID-scoped process name (`exec -a loa-cmp14-hung-$$`) and the probe looks for that; no `pgrep` → an explicit skip.
- **LOW** (confidence: high) `tests/unit/verdict-derive.bats` — no case pinned that an envelope without `rejected_summary` (every pre-FR-2.2 envelope) or with `null` passes (chunk c C-002). **Fix:** both pinned at exit 0 (the script already read the field as `// []`).
- **LOW** (confidence: high) `tests/unit/adversarial-review-companion.bats:CMP-16` — nothing asserted the raw token never reaches the a2a directory or survives in the workdir (chunk c C-004). **Fix:** CMP-16 greps `$OUT_DIR` and asserts no `/tmp/adversarial-<sprint>-*` workdir survives the EXIT trap; the resource doc says where the raw stderr lives.
- **LOW** (confidence: high) `tests/unit/adversarial-review-companion.bats:CMP-12` — the repair round-trip is intercepted only because it goes through `invoke_dissenter`; nothing asserted that (chunk c C-005). **Fix:** CMP-12 asserts two `claude-headless` calls (dissent + repair).
- **LOW** (confidence: high) process — two cheval invocations now append concurrently to `.run/cost-ledger.jsonl` and the MODELINV chain; whether the writers serialise was not stated (chunk c C-006). **Verified in code**, not changed: `metering/ledger.py` appends under `fcntl.flock(LOCK_EX)`; `audit_envelope.py` holds a flock on `<log>.lock` across `prev_hash` computation and append (issue #689). Documented in the resource.
- **LOW** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh:_fold_companion` — reviewer's own find from the live run: cheval reports its CLI-hop timeout ("`claude -p` timed out after 610s") as `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` / exit 1, so the class read `model_unavailable`, and `last_error` was the model-adapter shim's generic wrapper line. **Fix:** a diagnostic saying "timed out" classes as `timeout` when the exit code does not decide; `last_error` prefers the provider's line over the wrapper. CMP-19.
- **LOW** (confidence: medium) `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh:_fold_companion` — findings named only the outer hop as `voice`, although cheval's inner chain may have answered from another model (chunk c C-003, first half). **Fix:** every finding also carries `answered_by` (the primary's succeeded id, or the companion's model); CMP-18 asserts it.

### 7. Third two-voice run, on the round-1c commit (chunk a: one primary BLOCKING refuted, one companion BLOCKING + six advisories; chunk b six; chunk c four)

- **HIGH** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh:_fold_companion` — a FAILED companion whose id is one of the primary's succeeded voices still produced a dropped-voice envelope; on this host's likely shape (the primary falls through to `claude-headless`, the `claude-headless` companion times out) the aggregator's INV-5 rejects one id both succeeded and dropped and the whole `verdict_quality` is lost (chunk a C-001, BLOCKING). **Fix:** the failed branch compares the dropped id with the union of the primary attempts' succeeded ids; a collision writes no synthetic envelope and records `counted_as: duplicate_voice`. CMP-20 (aggregation survives; the ordinary failed companion is still a dropped voice).
- **MEDIUM** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh:_fold_companion` — independence and `answered_by` were judged from the companion's outer hop while the primary's side used its succeeded id (chunk a C-002). **Fix:** the companion's own last sidecar decides (`companion_voice.answered_by`); independence compares the two `answered_by` families. CMP-21.
- **MEDIUM** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh:main` — the wait cap was chain length × the HTTP `timeout_seconds` + 30, counted from the end of the primary walk, while a `*-headless` hop is bounded by cheval's 610 s CLI timeout (chunk a C-003). **Fix:** `_companion_wait_cap` sums each hop's own bound (CLI hops 610 s, `LOA_ADVERSARIAL_CLI_HOP_TIMEOUT`; HTTP hops `timeout_seconds`) + 30 s and the loop measures from the companion's start. CMP-22.
- **MEDIUM** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/verdict-derive.sh:rejected_summary_check` — with no envelope beside the feedback the check returned before walking the sidecars, so rows left by a run that died after writing them were silently lost (chunk b C-002). **Fix:** the sidecars are walked first; rows with no envelope are a violation naming them. Test added.
- **MEDIUM** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/verdict-derive.sh:rejected_summary_check` — `$(( rows + $(grep -c …) ))` aborted under `set -e` with no violation text when a sidecar was unreadable (chunk b C-005). **Fix:** the count is captured and defaulted; an unreadable sidecar is its own violation. Test added (skips when the user can read mode-000 files).
- **LOW** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh` — the cleanup trap was installed only when a companion was planned and replaced the original `rm -rf` EXIT trap (chunk a C-004). **Fix:** one `_adv_cleanup_on_exit` EXIT/INT/TERM trap where the workdir is created; `LOA_ADVERSARIAL_KEEP_WORKDIR=1` retains it.
- **LOW** (confidence: medium) `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh:_walk_companion_chain` — both walks read the same prompt files (chunk a C-005; the KF-011 debug capture is keyed by model + timestamp, so no other shared write was found). **Fix:** the companion reads its own copies in its sub-workdir.
- **LOW** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh:main` — the companion block assumed `_ADVERSARIAL_WORKDIR`; the `/dev/null/fold.log` fallback could not be opened (chunk a C-006). **Fix:** no workdir → `planned: false, reason: no_workdir`; `fold.log` lives in the companion sub-workdir.
- **LOW** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh:_companion_chain` — the default OpenAI-family companion chain led with `gpt-5.5-pro`, which KF-002 (recurrence 5) records as returning empty content on review prompts (chunk a C-007). **Fix:** the default starts at `gpt-5.5`; `companion_chain.openai` can still name the pro model; example, CHANGELOG and report say so. CMP-4.
- **LOW** (confidence: medium) `.claude/scripts/verdict-derive.sh:rejected_summary_check` — "the sidecar accumulates across rounds and repaired rows inflate the count" (chunk b C-001): partly refuted — every writer truncates its own sidecar at run start and only rejections are ever written; the per-chunk files in this directory are the chunk runner's copies, replaced per round. **Fix:** rows with `repair_succeeded: true` are excluded defensively and the resource says how the files behave; CMP-23 pins the per-run truncation.
- **LOW** (confidence: high) `.claude/skills/{reviewing-code,auditing-security}/SKILL.md` — the contract sentences did not name sidecar rows or top-level bullets (chunk b C-003). **Fix:** both now do, within budget (16,362 B / 16,376 B).
- **LOW** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/verdict-derive.sh` — `--json` after the offending token produced no JSON on a usage error (chunk b C-004). **Fix:** argv is pre-scanned for `--json`. Test added; consumers checked (`golden-path.sh`, `spiral-evidence.sh` treat every non-zero exit as not approved).
- **LOW** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/verdict-derive.sh:show_help` — two `(default: …)` paragraphs under `--envelope` (chunk b C-006). **Fix:** one paragraph.
- **LOW** (confidence: high) `tests/unit/adversarial-review-companion.bats:CMP-10` — the promoted envelope's verdict quality was unpinned (chunk c C-002). **Fix:** CMP-10 asserts `verdict_quality.status` is not APPROVED, the primary hop appears in `voices_dropped`, one voice succeeded, `primary_voice.error` and `status_note` are set. `metadata.status` stays `reviewed` with `degraded: true` — the convention the gate and the degraded-on-rejection path already use.
- **LOW** (confidence: high) `tests/unit/adversarial-review-companion.bats:CMP-12`, `tests/unit/adversarial-review-normalise.bats:NRM-4` — `wc -l` comparisons (BSD pads the count) (chunk c C-003). **Fix:** `grep -c ''`.
- **LOW** (confidence: high) `tests/unit/adversarial-review-companion.bats:CMP-16` — the recursive grep would pass vacuously on a missing a2a directory (chunk c C-004). **Fix:** `[ -d "$OUT_DIR" ]` first; the `/tmp` probe matches the script's own workdir path (not `mktemp`).
- **LOW** (confidence: high) `tests/unit/adversarial-review-companion.bats` — the sidecar's per-run semantics and row counting were unpinned (chunk c C-001). **Fix:** CMP-23 (two runs into one sprint directory → one row; a trailing blank line counts for nothing).

### 8. Fourth two-voice run, on the round-1d commit (chunk a: companion timed out with the CLI idle; chunk b: one primary + one companion BLOCKING, three advisories; chunk c: two BLOCKING, six advisories)

- **HIGH** (confidence: high) `.claude/adapters/loa_cheval/providers/headless_cli.py` / `.claude/defaults/model-config.yaml` — reviewer's own find from the live run: the `claude-headless` companion timed out at 612 s on a 36 KB chunk with nothing else on the CLI. cheval's headless adapter bounds every CLI hop at connect 10 s + max(provider `read_timeout`, 600 s); the dissent's `--timeout` never reaches it, and the model-adapter shim runs cheval with its stderr discarded, so the envelope said `model_unavailable` with a shim banner as `last_error`. **Fix:** `ModelConfig.headless_timeout_seconds` (per model, catalog: `claude-headless` 900 s — a dissent on `claude -p` takes 6–10 minutes; never lowers the floor); the dissent's wait cap reads that bound per `*-headless` hop; the failure diagnostic is read from the MODELINV ledger row cheval wrote for the call. Python contract test; CMP-22, CMP-24; KF-037 attempt row.
- **HIGH** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/verdict-derive.sh:rejected_summary_check` — with no envelope beside the feedback, sidecar rows produced a violation that no triage could clear: the branch returned before the section or its bullets were examined (chunk b C-001, BLOCKING). **Fix:** the envelope-less case falls through to the shared section / bullet count. Test.
- **HIGH** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/verdict-derive.sh` / `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh` — stale sidecars from a writer that did not run this time (companion off, `no_route`, a chunk suffix no longer produced) were demanded as triage beside a fresh envelope (chunk b C-003; chunk c C-002, BLOCKING). **Fix:** one run owns the directory's rejected set — both canonical sidecars are removed at start and the envelope lists the ones this run produced (`metadata.rejected_sidecars`); `verdict-derive.sh` counts only those when the envelope names them, the glob only for an envelope-less directory. CMP-23; verdict-derive test; the chunk merge script unions `rejected_summary` and lists the per-chunk sidecars.
- **HIGH** (confidence: high) `tests/unit/adversarial-review-normalise.bats:teardown` (and the companion suite) — the delete target was derived from `SPRINT`, assigned near the end of `setup()` after fallible steps; bats runs teardown on a failed setup, so an empty `SPRINT` would have pointed `find … -delete` at the tracked a2a root (chunk c C-001, BLOCKING). **Fix:** the sprint id is the first line of `setup()`, and both teardowns refuse any path that is not `*/a2a/sprint-{norm,comp}-*`.

- **MEDIUM** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh` — the repair round-trip called `claude-headless` outside the per-binary lock (chunk a C-002). **Fix:** `_adv_with_cli_lock` wraps both the dissent hops and the repair calls. CMP-25.

- **LOW** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh:_adv_invoke_hop` — a world-shared lock directory, no fallback when the lock file cannot be opened, fd 9 inherited by the whole child tree (chunk a C-005). **Fix:** a per-user directory under `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`/`$TMPDIR`, an open-fd fallback that runs unserialised, and the fd closed for the child.

### 15. Eleventh two-voice run, on the round-1k commit, six chunks (a1 three advisories; a2's companion timed out on a 51 KB half-diff and its primary raised one BLOCKING; b one companion BLOCKING + two advisories; c1 three advisories; c2 and d lost their companion to an account rate limit and the breaker — re-run on the next commit)

- **LOW** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh:load_adversarial_config` — the companion opt-out matched `false|no|off|0` case-sensitively while yq echoes the source spelling, so `False` / `NO` / `Off` kept the second chain on silently (chunk a1 C-001). **Fix:** the YAML boolean spellings match in any case; a non-boolean value is said and the voice stays on. - **LOW** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/verdict-derive.sh` — the trailer-less failure paths printed nothing on stdout in plain mode, so a status-line consumer could not tell the result from a crash (chunk b DISS-C-003). **Fix:** `INCONSISTENT: gate=<gate> verdict=none (no trailer)` before `exit 1` on both paths. s19.

### 16. Twelfth two-voice run, on the round-1l commit, seven chunks — two voices on every chunk, no BLOCKING (a1 three advisories; a2 six; a3 seven; b six; c1 three; c2 four; d two — 30 acted on, one refuted)

- **MEDIUM** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh:main` — the INV-5 pass excluded a whole primary attempt envelope when any of its dropped voices was one the companion succeeded with, so a MIXED envelope (cheval's inner walk dropped `claude-headless` and answered with `codex-headless`) lost the primary's own voice from `verdict_quality` (chunk a3 C-007). **Fix:** a copy without the conflicting dropped entries, `voices_planned` decremented (INV-6 holds; `single_voice_call` follows), is aggregated in its place — both passes; `primary_attempts_rewritten` / `companion_attempts_rewritten` recorded. CMP-39.

- **LOW** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh` — the repair-hop retirement read exit 1 as "unavailable", though exit 1 also covers a CLI-hop timeout and a transient HTTP failure whose provider line the round-trip does not keep (chunk a1 C-001). **Fix:** only the explicit auth (4) / quota (6) codes retire. - **MEDIUM** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh` (id pass) — a primary's explicit `DISS-C-NNN` id was kept and could equal a folded companion id (a1 C-003). **Fix:** the primary's pass reserves `^DISS-C-` (renumbered, `id_derived`). NRM-37.

Without `--since`, or with a newer envelope, the refusal stands and names `--since`. Every run now logs `run started <ts>`, because the skills' allowlists hold no `date`. CMP-127 / CMP-129.
- **MEDIUM** (confidence: high) `.claude/scripts/adversarial-review.sh:_adv_take_run_lock` — a run-lock `mkdir` failing for a reason other than EEXIST (EACCES / ENOSPC / EROFS) retried three times, then ran unguarded saying "did not settle". An unopenable takeover section file (`exec 7>>` failing) read as "another taker holds the takeover" and refused (a2 C-002). **Fix:** mkdir's error is captured, and a non-EEXIST failure goes unguarded at once with mkdir's reason. An unopenable section is the verdict `unopenable`, which runs unguarded with its reason and is never `busy`. CMP-131 (red first).
